Remarks.&mdash;During January the highest reading of the barometer was 30.490, on the 4th; and the lowest 29.850, on the 25th. The highest temperature was 83&deg;, on the 1st; and the lowest 370, on the 5th. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 3rd, averaging 15.2 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 10 days, measuring 2.33 inches. During February the highest reading of the barometer was 30.414, on the nth; and the lowest 29.700, on the 16th. The highest temperature was 83&deg;.5, on the 17th; and the lowest 43&deg;, on the nth. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 18th, averaging 13.6 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on n days, measuring 1.48 inches. During March the highest reading of the barometer was 30.390, on the 5th; and the lowest 29.870, on the 9th. The highest temperature was 86&deg;, on the 21st; and the lowest 420, on the 1st. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 23rd, averaging 13.8 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 22 days, measuring 4.66 inches. IX. The apparatus of the new light is fixed dioptric of the fourth order, and is arne o show sectors of white and red light between the following approximate bearings: White from N. 15 W. to N. 8 5 30&#39; W. BeAd from N. 85* 3o&#39;W. to S. 82* W. White from S. 82&#39; W. to S. 24* W. Bed from8. 24&#39; W. round by&#39;-outh to S. 87* E. White from 8. 87*E. to.N. 82* E. From N. 82&#39; E. round by north to N. 15* W. the light will be entirely obscured by Loka Island. The bearings are magnetic and token from eward; and until a further Notice is issued, the sectors are subject to alteration. Tfhe light will be elevated 128 feet above the sea, and in clear weather the white igh should be visible from a distance of I5 miles. 1v The tower is round, built of stone, with a total height from the base to the crown f the lantern of 29 feet. BUSH ISLAND AND TSUNGMING BANK BITJOYS. Nonms is hereby given that- I*. The Bush Island Buoy (No. 6 of the Shanghai district in&#39;the List of Lights, etc. for 1890) has been iremoved, the bank which formerly extended off the N.W. p oint of Bush Island, and which the buoy was placed to mark, having disappeared. 2*. Tsungming Bank Buoy.-A conical buoy, 6 feet in diameter at the water&#39;s edge, painted&#39;,red, and surmounted by a black spherical cage, has been moored inV 18 feet at low water on the north side of the channel between Bush and Tsungming Islands. It marks the southern edge of the shoals lying to the 8.W. of Tsungming Island, and vessels should not pass to the northward of it, as the bank is very steep. SnuGHAi, 17th February i 890.

224 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT.-DEC., (Received 24th March iSgo.) CUSTOM HOUSE, MfiNGTZU, 14th February 1890. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875,1 now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Mengtzu, Manhao, and Mapai-kuan for the fourth quarter of the year 1889. b.
